preserves the first column of the tribimaximal mixing
Harrison et al. 2002
. We use the
\text{TM}_{1}
parametrization presented in
Gautam 2018
,

\displaystyle U_{\text{TM}_{1}}(\theta,\zeta)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}&\frac{\cos\theta}{\sqrt{3}}&\frac{\sin\theta}{\sqrt{3}}\\[6.0pt]
\frac{-1}{\sqrt{6}}&\frac{\cos\theta}{\sqrt{3}}-\frac{\sin\theta}{\sqrt{2}}e^{i\zeta}&\frac{\sin\theta}{\sqrt{3}}+\frac{\cos\theta}{\sqrt{2}}e^{i\zeta}\\[6.0pt]
\frac{-1}{\sqrt{6}}&\frac{\cos\theta}{\sqrt{3}}+\frac{\sin\theta}{\sqrt{2}}e^{i\zeta}&\frac{\sin\theta}{\sqrt{3}}-\frac{\cos\theta}{\sqrt{2}}e^{i\zeta}\end{matrix}\right).

(79)

We express the neutrino mixing angles and the Dirac CP phase in terms of the
\text{TM}_{1}
parameters
\theta
and
\zeta
using the following equations,

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\theta_{13}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sin^{2}\theta}{3}\,,

(80)

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\theta_{12}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{2}{3-\sin^{2}\theta}\,,

(81)

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\theta_{23}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{6}\sin 2\theta\cos\zeta}{3-\sin^{2}\theta}\right)\,,

(82)

\displaystyle\begin{split}J_{\text{CP}}&=\text{im}(U_{e2}U_{\mu 3}U^{*}_{e3}U^{*}_{\mu 2})\\
&=\frac{1}{8}\sin\delta_{\text{CP}}\sin 2\theta_{12}\sin 2\theta_{23}\sin 2\theta_{13}\cos\theta_{13}\\
&=\frac{\sin 2\theta\sin\zeta}{6\sqrt{6}},\end{split}

(83)

where
J_{\text{CP}}
is the Jarlkog’s rephasing invariant
Jarlskog 1985a
;
Jarlskog 1985b
, which parametrizes CP violation.

\text{TM}_{1}
mixing scheme with
\theta=\frac{\pi}{12}
and
\zeta=\frac{\pi}{2}
was proposed in
Zhou 2012
to account for the nonzero reactor angle. Ref.
Krishnan 2022
presented a model in the framework of the auxiliary group that resulted in
U_{\text{TM}_{1}}\!({\scriptstyle\frac{\pi}{12},\,\text{-}\frac{\pi}{2}})
. Substituting
\theta=\frac{\pi}{12}
and
\zeta=-\frac{\pi}{2}
in (
80
)-(
83
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\theta_{13}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\sin^{2}\frac{\pi}{12}=0.0223\,,

(84)

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\theta_{12}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{2}{3-\sin^{2}\frac{\pi}{12}}=0.318\,,

(85)

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\theta_{23}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\,,

(86)

\displaystyle J_{\text{CP}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{12\sqrt{6}},\quad\delta_{\text{CP}}=-\frac{\pi}{2}.

(87)

Our predictions (
84
) and (
85
) are within
1\sigma
errors of the global fit
de Salas et al. 2021
values given by
\sin^{2}\theta_{13}=0.022000^{+0.00069}_{-0.00062}
and
\sin^{2}\theta_{12}=0.318^{+16}_{-16}
. Since our model preserves
\mu\tau
-reflection symmetry
Harrison and Scott 2002a
;
Babu et al. 2003
;
Ma 2002
;
Harrison and Scott 2002b
;
Grimus and Lavoura 2004
, we obtain maximal atmospheric mixing (
\sin^{2}\theta_{23}=\frac{1}{2}
) and maximal CP violation (
\delta_{\text{CP}}=-\frac{\pi}{2}
). These predictions are in tension with the global fit at
2\sigma
level even though they are consistent at
3\sigma
level
de Salas et al. 2021
given by
\sin^{2}\theta_{23}=0.434-0.610
and
\delta_{\text{CP}}=0.71\pi-1.99\pi
.

Appendix D
The renormalizable flavon potential

We follow the procedure developed in
Krishnan 2023
to construct the general renormalizable flavon potential. In this procedure, we infer the existence of specific minima of the potential using symmetry arguments without the need for explicit extremization of the potential. These arguments utilize theorems by Michel
Michel 1971
;
Michel and Zhilinskii 2001
, reduction lemma by Golubitsky and Stewart
Golubitsky et al. 1988
and the compatibility condition obtained by the author
Krishnan 2023
.

We analyze the potentials of the elementary flavons one by one. We show that the potentials of
\chi
and
\xi_{\mathrm{a}}
have the required minima. On the other hand, the potentials constructed with the rest of the flavons have accidental continuous symmetries. To break the continuous symmetries for each of these elementary flavons, we introduce driving flavons. We construct the potential of these driving flavons and obtain its minimum, and then construct the cross terms of the elementary flavon with the driving flavons. These cross terms break the continuous symmetries, resulting in the required discrete minimum for the flavon. Once this procedure is completed for all the elementary flavons, we comprehensively search for all possible cross terms among the flavons, including the driving flavons. We show that all cross terms are compatible
Krishnan 2023
with the stabilizers of the individual flavons; hence, their presence does not spoil the minimum obtained earlier. Thus, by the end of this section, we will have constructed the general renormalizable potential
\mathcal{V}
involving all the flavons and shown that it has a minimum that corresponds to our vevs (
.3
).

The stabilizers associated with the elementary flavons as well as those associated with driving flavons, which we will identify in this section, remain unaffected by a general change in the coefficients of terms in
\mathcal{V}
. These stabilizers generate constraints among the components of the vevs. For example,
\tilde{H}_{\chi}=S_{3\chi}
, TABLE
6
, generates five constraints among the components of
\langle\chi\rangle
(
.3
). Since the stabilizers are coefficient-independent, the resulting constraints are also coefficient-independent even though the norms of the vevs change as a function of the coefficients. Such constraints were named homogeneous linear intrinsic constraints (HLICs) in
Krishnan 2023
. The predictions we make in Section
.4
are the result of the HLICs in our vevs (
.3
).

D.1
The potential involving
\chi

Using the quadratic invariant
|\chi|^{2}
and the quartic invariant
|\chi|^{4}
, we construct

\text{\small$\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\chi}=c_{1}m^{2}|\chi|^{2}+c_{2}|\chi|^{4}$}.

(88)

For
c_{2}>0
and
c_{1}<0
, the minimum of this potential is a
5
-sphere. This can be made manifest by rewriting
\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\chi}
as

\text{\small$\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\chi}=k_{1}(|\chi|^{2}-r_{\!\chi}^{2})^{2}-k_{1}r_{\!\chi}^{4}$}\,,

(89)

with
r_{\!\chi}
and
k_{1}
being positive real numbers. The above potential has a minimum when
|\chi|=r_{\!\chi}
. Here,
r_{\!\chi}
forms the radius of the
5
-sphere. By comparing (
88
) and (
89
), we can express
r_{\!\chi}
and
k_{1}
as functions of
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
. We call
r_{\!\chi}
and
k_{1}
the arbitrary constants, and
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
the coefficients. In
Krishnan 2023
, we denoted the arbitrary constants as functions of the coefficients explicitly, e.g.,
r_{\!\chi}(c_{1},c_{2})
and
k_{1}(c_{1},c_{2})
. In this paper, we do not show their functional dependence on the coefficients for the sake of brevity, e.g.,
r_{\!\chi}
and
k_{1}
.

The symmetry group
6
6

6

In this Appendix, we use the notation introduced in
Krishnan 2023
.
\breve{T}
denotes the continuous symmetry group of
Transitive
action on the stationary manifold (minimum).
\acute{G}
denotes the group obtained by the breaking of
\breve{T}
by the newly added terms.
\acute{H}
and
\acute{S}
denote the pointwise and setwise stabilizers, respectively, of the minimum of the newly obtained potential. When the multiplet is an irrep, the symmetry groups associated with the minimum are given by
\tilde{H}=\acute{H}
and
\tilde{T}=\acute{S}/\acute{H}
. Please see Section C and FIG. 3 in
Krishnan 2023
for more details.
of the minimum of

, i.e., the

\breve{T}_{\chi}=O(6)_{\chi}
. We break the continous group
\breve{T}_{\chi}
to the discrete group

to

(

),

\text{\small$\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\chi}=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\chi}+c_{3}m\,\text{re}(\chi^{\scriptscriptstyle T}\chi^{2\zeta})+c_{4}|\chi^{2\delta}|^{2}$}.

(90)

The alignment
\langle\chi\rangle
(
.3
) breaks
\acute{G}_{\chi}
to its stabilizer
\acute{H}_{\chi}=S_{3\chi}
, TABLE

. Every degree of freedom of

Michel 1971

\text{\small$\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\chi}=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\chi}+k_{2}|\chi^{2\zeta}\!+\frac{r_{\!\chi}}{\sqrt{3}}\chi^{*}|^{2}+k_{3}|\chi^{2\delta}|^{2}+\frac{k_{2}}{3}|\chi^{2\alpha}|^{2}$},

(91)

where

has the form (
89

,
\chi^{2\zeta}=-\frac{r_{\!\chi}}{\sqrt{3}}\chi^{*}

\chi^{2\delta}=0
and
\chi^{2\alpha}=0
. It can be shown that these equations have a discrete set of solutions, and

is one among them. (

and

only. (

,

,

,

†
footnotemark:

, we have
\tilde{H}_{\chi}=\acute{H}_{\chi}=S_{3\chi}
.

It can be shown that among the various quartic invariants that we can construct, only three are linearly independent. In (
90
), we have utilized two. We construct the general renormalizable potential by adding
|\chi^{2\alpha}|^{2}
as the third quartic invariant to (
90
),

\text{\small$\mathcal{V}_{\chi}=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\chi}+c_{5}|\chi^{2\alpha}|^{2}$}.

(92)

|\chi^{2\alpha}|^{2}
is compatible
Krishnan 2023
with
\tilde{H}_{\chi}
since it remains invariant it. Hence, even after the addition of
|\chi^{2\alpha}|^{2}
the minimum of the potential continues to have
\tilde{H}_{\chi}
as its stabilizer, i.e., the constraints among the components of
\langle\chi\rangle
, which are generated by
\tilde{H}_{\chi}
, remain unaffected by the addition of

. We make this manifest by rewriting

\text{\small$\mathcal{V}_{\chi}=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\chi}+k_{4}|\chi^{2\alpha}|^{2}$},

91
).
\mathcal{V}_{\chi}

\langle\chi\rangle
.
\mathcal{V}_{\chi}
(

) contains five arbitrary constants

, which can be expressed as functions of the five coefficients

present in
\mathcal{V}_{\chi}
(
92
). Even though the norm of the vev,
r_{\!\chi}
, is a function of the coefficients, the constraints among the components of the vev remain unaffected by a general variation of the coefficients.

We are able to rewrite the general potential (
92
) in the form (
93
) in terms of the arbitrary constants to make
\langle\chi\rangle
manifest precisely because the symmetries of
\langle\chi\rangle
guarantee its existence as a stationary point of the potential. In the rest of this Appendix, we provide the potentials constructed with the various flavons in two forms: the first form similar to (

) and the second form similar to (

) containing the arbitrary constants (the norms and

). The number of arbitrary constants will always be equal to the number of coefficients and we will always be able to express the arbitrary constants as functions of the coefficients.

). This potential attains minimum when
|\xi_{\mathrm{b}}|=r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}
and
{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha}=0
. Every point in the manifold
\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(e^{i\theta_{1}},e^{i\theta_{2}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
satisfies these two equations. The arbitrary constants
r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}},k_{7}
and
k_{8}
in (

) can be obtained as functions of the coefficients
c_{9},c_{10}
and
c_{11}
in (
100
). The pointwise stabilizer of the manifold
\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(e^{i\theta_{1}},e^{i\theta_{2}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
under
\acute{G}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}
is
\acute{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}=1
8

, i.e., we have
\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}=1
. The group of transitive action on the manifold is
\tilde{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}=\acute{G}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}=(O(2)_{1}\times O(2)_{2}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}

Table 9:
The transformation rules for the driving flavons
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
and the irreps obtained from them.

To break the continuous group
\tilde{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}
, we introduce an auxiliary generator, namely
\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{\mu}}
, and two diving flavons
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}=({\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1},{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}=({\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1},{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
, which transform under it as well as the other generators introduced in our model. How these driving flavons transform is given in TABLE
9
. We define
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
as a complex doublet, which is, in fact, a real irrep of dimension four, i.e.,
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}\equiv(\mu_{\mathrm{p}},\mu_{\mathrm{p}}^{*})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
. On the other hand,
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
is defined as a real irrep of dimension two. The tensor products relevant to our analysis are given below

\mu_{\mathrm{p}}\otimes\mu_{\mathrm{p}}

=|\mu_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}\oplus{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}\oplus{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}\oplus{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\gamma}\oplus{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta},

(102)

\mu_{\mathrm{q}}\otimes\mu_{\mathrm{q}}

=|\mu_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2}\oplus{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha}\oplus{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta},

(103)

\mu_{\mathrm{p}}\otimes\mu_{\mathrm{q}}

\displaystyle\text{\small$={\mu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}\oplus{\mu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\beta}$}\,,

(104)

where

{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}

\displaystyle\text{\small$={\mu_{\mathrm{p}}^{*}}_{1}{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}-{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}^{*}}_{2}{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}$},

{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}

\displaystyle\text{\small$={\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}^{\!\!*}{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}$},

(105)

{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\gamma}

\displaystyle\text{\small$={\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}$},

{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}

\displaystyle\text{\small$=({\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}^{\!\!2},{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}^{\!\!2})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}$},

(106)

{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha}

\displaystyle\text{\small$={\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1}^{\!2}-{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2}^{\!2}$},

{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!2\beta}

\displaystyle\text{\small$={\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2}$},

(107)

{\mu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}

=({\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}^{\!\!*}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1},{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}^{\!\!*}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2})^{\scriptscriptstyle T},

{\mu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\beta}

\displaystyle\text{\small$=({\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}^{\!\!*}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2},{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}^{\!\!*}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}$}.\!\!\!

(108)

How these irreps transform is also given in TABLE

We can see that
{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}

{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}
, TABLE

. By coupling these together using the cross term
|{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}-\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{2}}{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}|^{2}
, we can break
\tilde{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}
. For
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
, we assign the alignment

\langle\mu_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle=\frac{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(\omega,\bar{\omega})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}.

(109)

Its stabilizer
K_{4\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}
, TABLE
10
, fully determines it up to its norm. Hence, it is guaranteed by symmetry arguments to be a stationary point of the potential of
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
. At
\langle\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\rangle
(
.3
) and
\langle\mu_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle
(
109
), the cross term
|{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}-\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{2}}{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}|^{2}
vanishes.

We construct the potentials of
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}

=c_{12}m^{2}|\mu_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}+c_{13}|\mu_{\mathrm{p}}|^{4}

(110)

\displaystyle\text{\small$=k_{9}(|\mu_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}-r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2})^{2}-k_{9}r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{4}$}.

(111)

This potential has a
3
-sphere with radius

), we construct the general potential of
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
,

\mathcal{V}_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}

=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}+c_{14}({\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha})^{2}

\displaystyle\text{\small$=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}+k_{10}({\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha})^{2}$}.

(113)

The newly added term breaks the
\breve{T}_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}
to
\acute{G}_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}=(O(2)_{1}\times O(2)_{2}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}

.

(

(

|{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}-\frac{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}}{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}}{\mu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}|^{2}
vanishes.

Table 11:
The action of

,

To make these arguments clear, we construct the potentials explicitly. To aid our construction, we define the group element
\mathbf{E}_{\mu}=\mathbf{Z}_{\nu}^{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{\nu}\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{\mu}})^{2}
. We can show that
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
change sign under
\mathbf{E}_{\mu}
while every other flavon remains invariant under it, TABLE
11
. Therefore,
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
can couple with other flavons via quadratic products only, which we obtained in (
102
)-(
104
). Using
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
, we construct

\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}

=c_{15}m^{2}|\mu_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2}+c_{16}|\mu_{\mathrm{q}}|^{4}

(115)

\displaystyle\text{\small$=k_{11}(|\mu_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2}-r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{2})^{2}-k_{11}r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{4}$}.

(116)

This potential has a circle with radius
r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}
as its minimum, having the continuous symmetry
\breve{T}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}=O(2)_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}
. The general potential of
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
is

\mathcal{V}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}

=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}+c_{17}({\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha})^{2}

(117)

\displaystyle\text{\small$=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}+k_{12}({\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha})^{2}$}.

(118)

The newly added term breaks the
\breve{T}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}
to
\acute{G}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}=D_{8\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}
, the dihedral group of eight elements generated by the group actions
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!x}\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!z}\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
. The vev
\langle\mu_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle
(
114
) breaks
\acute{G}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}
to
\acute{H}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}=Z_{2\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}
, TABLE
10
. The stabilizer
\acute{H}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}
fully determines
\langle\mu_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle
in the circle; hence,
\langle\mu_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle
is guaranteed to be a stationary point of
\mathcal{V}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}
(
117
). The rewritten potential (
118
) shows that it can be obtained as a minimum. At the minimum of (
118
), we have
|\mu_{\mathrm{q}}|=r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}
and
{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}=0
, which corresponds to
\langle\mu_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle
. The arbitrary constants
r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}},k_{11}
and
k_{12}
in (
118
) are functions of the coefficients
c_{15},c_{16}
and
c_{17}
(
117
). We have
\tilde{H}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}=\acute{H}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}=Z_{2\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}
. We also have
\tilde{T}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}=1
since the minimum is a single point rather than a manifold.

Utilizing both
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
, we construct

\text{\small$\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}=\mathcal{V}_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}+\mathcal{V}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}$}.

(119)

Here,
\mu
denotes the irreps
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
combined, i.e.,
\mu=(\mu_{\mathrm{p}},\mu_{\mathrm{q}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
. This potential has a minimum consisting of the manifold
\frac{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(e^{i\theta_{1}},e^{i\theta_{2}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
for
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
and the point
\frac{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(1,1)^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
for
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
. The corresponding symmetry groups are
\breve{H}_{\mu}=\tilde{H}_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}\times\tilde{H}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}=\tilde{H}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}=Z_{2\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}
and
\breve{T}_{\mu}=\tilde{T}_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}\times\tilde{T}_{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}=\tilde{T}_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}=(O(2)_{1}\times O(2)_{2}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}
. The continuous group
\breve{T}_{\mu}
is broken by the cross term between
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
,

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}+c_{18}\,\text{re}({\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta\dagger}{\mu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}),

(120)

to the discrete group
\acute{G}_{\mu}=(D_{6_{1}}\times D_{6_{2}}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}
generated by the group actions
{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}\rightarrow\omega{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}
,
{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}\rightarrow{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{*}_{1}
,
{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}\rightarrow\omega{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}
,
{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}\rightarrow{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{*}_{2}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!x}\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
. The manifold
\frac{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(e^{i\theta_{1}},e^{i\theta_{2}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
gets stratified under
\acute{G}_{\mu}
. Our vev
\langle\mu_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle=\frac{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(\omega,\bar{\omega})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
(
109
) is a point in the manifold, and it breaks
\acute{G}_{\mu}
to its stabilizer
\acute{H}_{\mu}=(K_{4}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}
, generated by
{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}\rightarrow\bar{\omega}{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}^{*}
,
{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}\rightarrow\omega{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}^{*}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!x}\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
. Since the stabilizer
\acute{H}_{\mu}
fully determines
\langle\mu_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle
in the manifold, symmetry arguments guarantee that it is a stationary point of the potential. To show that it can be obtained as a minimum, we rewrite
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}
(
120
) as

\begin{split}\text{\small$\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}$}&\text{\small$=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}+k_{13}|{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}-\frac{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}}{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}}{\mu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}|^{2}$}\\
&\quad\text{\small$+\frac{k_{13}}{4}({\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}-\frac{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{2}}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha})^{2}+\frac{k_{13}}{4}(|\mu_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}\!-\frac{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{2}}|\mu_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2})^{2}.\!\!\!$}\end{split}

(121)

The above potential contains seven arbitrary constants
r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}},r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}},k_{9},..,k_{13}
, which can be expressed as functions of the seven coefficients
c_{12},..,c_{18}
. Since the arbitrary constants are all positive, the potential attains its minimum when the various terms in it vanish. It can be shown that they do vanish at our vevs
\langle\mu_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle
(
109
) and
\langle\mu_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle
(
114
). For the combined vev
\langle\mu\rangle=(\langle\mu_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle,\langle\mu_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle)^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
, we have
\tilde{H}_{\mu}=\acute{H}_{\mu}\times\breve{H}_{\mu}=(K_{4}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}\times Z_{2\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}
. We also have
\tilde{T}_{\mu}=1
.

The stabilizer associated with
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
that we obtained here, i.e.,
(K_{4}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}
, is different from that we provided in TABLE
10
, i.e.,
K_{4\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}
. In TABLE
10
, we listed the stabilizers corresponding to the various flavons under the assumption that the potentials of these flavons do not contain accidental symmetries. In reality, for many flavons, such as
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
, the renormalizable potentials do contain accidental continuous symmetries; hence, we introduce other flavons, such as
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
, to break them. In such a scenario, we may obtain a stabilizer different from what we expect in relation to the potential of the original flavon alone, e.g.,
(K_{4}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}})\neq K_{4\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}
. However, the fixed point subspace
Krishnan 2023
remains unique, e.g.,
\text{fix}((K_{4}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}})=\text{fix}(K_{4\mu_{\mathrm{p}}})
which is nothing but the space of all points of the form
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}=a(\omega,\bar{\omega})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
where
a
is a real number.

The general potential of
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
is given by

\mathcal{V}_{\mu}=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}+c_{19}{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}\!+c_{20}|\mu_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}|\mu_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2}

(122)

Since
{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}
and
{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}
vanish at our vev
\langle\mu\rangle
, i.e., at (
109
) and (
114
), the term
{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}
is compatible with
\tilde{H}_{\mu}
, according to corollary B in
Krishnan 2023
. Note that corollary B in
Krishnan 2023
was the main result in
Krishnan 2021
. The term
|\mu_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}|\mu_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2}
is compatible since it is a product of norms. Therefore, the addition of the above two terms does not spoil
\langle\mu\rangle
, i.e.,
\tilde{H}_{\mu}
remains unaffected. To make this manifest, we may rewrite (
122
) as

\text{\small$\mathcal{V}_{\mu}=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}+k_{14}({\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}-\frac{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{2}}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha})^{2}+k_{15}(|\mu_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}-\frac{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{2}}|\mu_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2})^{2}$},

(123)

where
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu}
has the form (
121
).
\mathcal{V}_{\mu}
(
123
) contains nine arbitrary constants
r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}},r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}},k_{9},..,k_{15}
, which are functions of the nine coeffiecients
c_{12},..,c_{20}
present in (
122
).

We combine the potentials
\mathcal{V}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\mu}
to obtain

\text{\small$\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}=\mathcal{V}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}+\mathcal{V}_{\mu}$},

(124)

where
\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu
denotes the combined multiplet of
\xi_{\mathrm{b}}
,
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
, i.e.,
\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu=(\xi_{\mathrm{b}},\mu_{\mathrm{p}},\mu_{\mathrm{q}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
. This potential contains
12
arbitrary constants and
12
coefficients. It has a minimum consisting of the manifold
\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(e^{i\theta_{1}},e^{i\theta_{2}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
for
\xi_{\mathrm{b}}
, the point
\frac{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(\omega,\bar{\omega})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
for
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
and the point
\frac{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(1,1)^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
for
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
. The symmetry groups of this minimum are
\breve{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}\!=\!\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}\!\times\!\tilde{H}_{\mu}\!=\!\tilde{H}_{\mu}\!=\!(K_{4}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}\!\times\!Z_{2\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}
and
\breve{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}\!=\!\tilde{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}\!\times\!\tilde{T}_{\mu}\!=\!\tilde{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}\!=\!(O(2)_{1}\times O(2)_{2}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}
. To break the continuous group
\breve{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
, we couple
\xi_{\mathrm{b}}
with
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
as given below,

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}

=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}+c_{21}\text{re}({\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta\dagger}{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta})

(125)

\displaystyle\text{\small$=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}+k_{16}|{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}-\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{2}}{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}|^{2}\!\!$}.

(126)

The newly added term breaks
\breve{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
to
\acute{G}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}=(K_{4_{1}}\times K_{4_{2}}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}
generated by the group actions
{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{1}\rightarrow-{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{1}
,
{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{1}\rightarrow\bar{\omega}{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{*}_{1}
,
{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{2}\rightarrow-{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{2}
,
{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{2}\rightarrow\omega{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{*}_{2}
and
\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!x}\xi_{\mathrm{b}}
. The manifold
\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(e^{i\theta_{1}},e^{i\theta_{2}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
gets stratified under
\acute{G}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
. Our vev
\langle\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\rangle
(
.3
) breaks
\acute{G}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
to its stabilizer
\acute{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}=(K_{4}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}
generated by the group actions
{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{1}\rightarrow\bar{\omega}{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{1}^{*}
,
{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{2}\rightarrow\omega{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{2}^{*}
and
\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{\omega}_{2}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!x}\xi_{\mathrm{b}}
. The stabilizer
(K_{4}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}
fully determines
\langle\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\rangle
in the manifold; hence,
\langle\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\rangle
is guaranteed to be a stationary point of
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
. The rewritten form of
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
(
126
) shows that
\langle\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\rangle
can be obtained as its minimum. The potentials (
125
) and (
126
) contain
13
coefficients and
13
arbitrary constants, respectively. We also obtain
\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}=\acute{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}\times\breve{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}=(K_{4}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}\times(K_{4}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}\times Z_{2\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}
. In this analysis, we obtain the stabilizer associated with
\xi_{\mathrm{b}}
as
(K_{4}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}
. This group is different from
K_{4\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}
, TABLE
6
. However, we have
\text{fix}((K_{4}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}})=\text{fix}(K_{4\xi_{\mathrm{b}}})
.

There are some more cross terms between
\xi_{\mathrm{b}}
and the driving flavons that we have not yet included in the potential. By including those terms also, we construct the general potential containing
\xi_{\mathrm{b}}
,
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{\small${\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}$}&\text{\small$=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}+c_{22}\,\text{re}({\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta\dagger}{\mu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha})+c_{23}{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}$}\\
&\text{\small$\hskip 9.24994pt\,\,+c_{24}{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}+c_{25}|\xi_{\mathrm{b}}|^{2}|\mu_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}\!+c_{26}|\xi_{\mathrm{b}}|^{2}|\mu_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2}$}.\!\!\!\end{split}

(127)

Let us verify if the newly added cross terms are compatible with
\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
. The
25^{\text{th}}
and
26^{\text{th}}
terms are compatible since they are products of norms. It is straightforward to show that
{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}
,
{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}
and
{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}
vanish at
\langle\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\rangle
(
.3
),
\langle\mu_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle
(
109
) and
\langle\mu_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle
(
114
). Hence, by corollary B
Krishnan 2023
, the
23^{\text{rd}}
and
24^{\text{th}}
terms are compatible. To verify if the
22^{\text{nd}}
term is compatible with
\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
, we need to utilize the general result involving the gradient and the fixed-point subspace given in equation (15) in
Krishnan 2023
. We may denote a point in the space of
\xi_{\mathrm{b}}
,
\mu_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{q}}
as

\text{\small$\big(({\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{1r},{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{1i},{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{2r},{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{2i}),({\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1r},{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1i},{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2r},{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2i}),({\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1},{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2})\big)^{\scriptscriptstyle T}$},

(128)

where
{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{1r}=\text{re}({\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{1})
,
{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{1i}=\text{im}({\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{1})
and so on. In the above basis, a general point in
\text{fix}(\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu})
is given by

\begin{split}&\text{\small$\big(\frac{a}{\sqrt{2}}(\frac{-1}{2},\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},\frac{-1}{2},\frac{-\sqrt{3}}{2}),$}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{\small$\frac{b}{\sqrt{2}}(\frac{-1}{2},\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},\frac{-1}{2},\frac{-\sqrt{3}}{2}),\!\frac{c}{\sqrt{2}}(1,1)\big)^{\!{\scriptscriptstyle T}}$}\end{split}

(129)

where
a
,
b
and
c
are real numbers. The expression of the
22^{\text{nd}}
term is given by

\begin{split}\text{\small$\text{re}({\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta\dagger}{\mu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha})$}&\text{\small$=({\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!2}_{1r}-{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!2}_{1i}){\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1r}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1}-2{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{1r}{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{1i}{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1i}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1}$}\\
&\quad\text{\small$-2{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{2r}{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}_{2i}{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2i}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2}+({\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!2}_{2r}-{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!2}_{2i}){\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2r}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2}$}.\end{split}

(130)

Differentiating (
130
) with the components of (
128
) and substituting the values from (
129
), we obtain the gradient of the cross term
\text{re}({\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta\dagger}{\mu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha})
at
\text{fix}(\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu})
as

\begin{split}&\text{\small$\big(\frac{abc}{\sqrt{2}}(\frac{-1}{2},\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},\frac{-1}{2},\frac{-\sqrt{3}}{2}),$}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad\text{\small$\frac{a^{2}c}{2\sqrt{2}}(\frac{-1}{2},\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},\frac{-1}{2},\frac{-\sqrt{3}}{2}),\frac{a^{2}b}{2\sqrt{2}}(1,1)\big)^{\scriptscriptstyle T}$}\!\!\!.\end{split}

(131)

The above expression can be obtained from (
129
) with the substitution
a\rightarrow abc
,
b\rightarrow\frac{a^{2}c}{2}
and
c\rightarrow\frac{a^{2}b}{2}
. In other words, the gradient of the term
\text{re}({\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta\dagger}{\mu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha})
calculated at every point in
\text{fix}(\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu})
lies in
\text{fix}(\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu})
. Hence,
\text{re}({\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta\dagger}{\mu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha})
is compatible with
\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
.

Since all the added terms are compatible with
\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
, the newly obtained potential
{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
(
127
) continues to have a minimum of the form
\langle\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\rangle
(
.3
),
\langle\mu_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle
(
109
) and
\langle\mu_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle
(
114
) with the symmetry group
\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
. This is made manifest by rewriting
{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{\small${\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}$}&=\text{\small$\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}+k_{17}|{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}-\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{2}}{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}}{\mu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}|^{2}\!\!$}\\
&\quad\,\text{\small$+k_{18}({\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}-\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{2}}{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}{\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha})^{2}+k_{19}({\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}-\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{2}}{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{2}}{\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha})^{2}$}\\
&\quad\,\text{\small$+k_{20}(|\xi_{\mathrm{b}}|^{2}\!-\!\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{2}}{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}|\mu_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2})^{2}\!+k_{21}(|\xi_{\mathrm{b}}|^{2}\!-\!\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{2}}{r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{2}}|\mu_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2})^{2}$},\!\!\!\end{split}

(132)

where
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
has the form (
126
).
{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}\mu}
(
127
) contains
18
coefficients
c_{9},..,c_{26}
. This matches with the number of arbitrary constants
r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{b}}},r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{p}}},r_{\!\mu_{\mathrm{q}}},k_{7},..,k_{21}
in (
132
), which are functions of these coefficients.

D.4
The potential involving
\phi_{\mathrm{b}}

Using
\phi_{\mathrm{b}}
, we construct

\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}

=c_{27}m^{2}|\phi_{\mathrm{b}}|^{2}+c_{28}|\phi_{\mathrm{b}}|^{4}

(133)

\displaystyle\text{\small$=k_{22}(|\phi_{\mathrm{b}}|^{2}-r_{\!\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{2})^{2}-k_{22}r_{\!\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{4}$},

(134)

which has a
3
-sphere with radius
r_{\!\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}
as its minimum. We have
\breve{T}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}=O(4)_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}
. The general renormalizable potential of
\phi_{\mathrm{b}}
is given by

\mathcal{V}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}

=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}+c_{29}({\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha})^{2}

(135)

\displaystyle\text{\small$=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}+k_{23}({\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha})^{2}$}.

(136)

Similar to the case of
\mathcal{V}_{\xi_{\mathrm{b}}}
(
100
), (
101
), the added term breaks
\breve{T}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}
to
\acute{G}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}=(O(2)_{1}\times O(2)_{2}\rtimes Z_{2})_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}
.
\mathcal{V}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}
has the manifold
\frac{r_{\!\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(e^{i\theta_{1}},e^{i\theta_{2}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
as its minimum with the symmetry groups
\tilde{H}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}=\acute{H}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}=1
and
\tilde{T}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}=\acute{G}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}
.

To break the continuous group
\tilde{T}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}
, we introduce an auxiliary generator
\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{\nu}}
and three driving flavons: a complex multiplet
\nu_{\mathrm{p}}=({\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1},{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
and two real doublets
\nu_{\mathrm{q}}=({\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1},{\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
and
\nu_{\mathrm{r}}=({\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}_{1},{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}_{2})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
that transform under
\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{\nu}}
and other generators in the model. The multiplet
\nu_{\mathrm{p}}
is a four-component real irrep
\nu_{\mathrm{p}}\equiv(\nu_{\mathrm{p}},\nu_{\mathrm{p}}^{*})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
. How the driving flavons transform is given in TABLE
12
. To help the construction of the potentials, we define a group element
\mathbf{E}_{\nu}=\mathbf{Z}_{\nu}^{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{\nu}\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{\nu}})^{2}
. Since
\nu_{\mathrm{p}}
,
\nu_{\mathrm{q}}
and
\nu_{\mathrm{r}}
are the only flavons that transform nontrivially (change of sign) under
\mathbf{E}_{\nu}
, TABLE
11
, they can appear only quadratically in cross terms. The quadratic tensor products involving
\nu_{\mathrm{p}}
,
\nu_{\mathrm{q}}
and
\nu_{\mathrm{r}}
are given below

\nu_{\mathrm{p}}\otimes\nu_{\mathrm{p}}

=|\nu_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}\!\oplus\!{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha}\!\oplus\!{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!2\beta}\oplus{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!2\gamma}\oplus{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!2\delta},

(137)

\nu_{\mathrm{q}}\otimes\nu_{\mathrm{q}}

=|\nu_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2}\oplus{\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha}\oplus{\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!2\beta},

(138)

\nu_{\mathrm{r}}\otimes\nu_{\mathrm{r}}

=|\nu_{\mathrm{r}}|^{2}\oplus{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!2\alpha}\oplus{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!2\beta},

(139)

\nu_{\mathrm{p}}\otimes\nu_{\mathrm{q}}

={\nu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}\oplus{\nu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\beta},

(140)

\nu_{\mathrm{p}}\otimes\nu_{\mathrm{r}}

={\nu_{\mathrm{pr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}\oplus{\nu_{\mathrm{pr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\beta},

(141)

\nu_{\mathrm{q}}\otimes\nu_{\mathrm{r}}

={\nu_{\mathrm{qr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}\oplus{\nu_{\mathrm{qr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!2\beta},

(142)

where

{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}

\displaystyle\text{\small$={\nu_{\mathrm{p}}^{*}}_{1}{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}-{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}^{*}}_{2}{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}$},

{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}

\displaystyle\text{\small$={\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}^{\!\!*}{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}$},

(143)

{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\gamma}

\displaystyle\text{\small$={\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}$},

{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}

\displaystyle\text{\small$=({\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}^{\!\!2},{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}^{\!\!2})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}$},

(144)

{\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha}

={\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1}^{\!2}-{\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2}^{\!2},

{\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!2\beta}

\displaystyle\text{\small$={\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1}{\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2}$},

(145)

{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!2\alpha}

\displaystyle\text{\small$={\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}_{1}^{\!2}-{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}_{2}^{\!2}$},

{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!2\beta}

\displaystyle\text{\small$={\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}_{1}{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}_{2}$},

(146)

{\nu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}

=({\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}^{\!\!*}{\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1},{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}^{\!\!*}{\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2})^{\scriptscriptstyle T},

{\nu_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\beta}

\displaystyle\text{\small$=({\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}^{\!\!*}{\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2},{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}^{\!\!*}{\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}$},\!\!\!

(147)

{\nu_{\mathrm{pr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}

=({\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}^{\!\!*}{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}_{1},{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}^{\!\!*}{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}_{2})^{\scriptscriptstyle T},

{\nu_{\mathrm{pr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\beta}

\displaystyle\text{\small$=({\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1}^{\!\!*}{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}_{2},{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2}^{\!\!*}{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}_{1})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}$},\!\!\!

(148)

{\nu_{\mathrm{qr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}

=({\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1}{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}_{1},{\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2}{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}_{2})^{\scriptscriptstyle T},

{\nu_{\mathrm{qr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!2\beta}

\displaystyle\text{\small$=({\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1}{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}_{2},{\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2}{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}_{1})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}$}.\!\!\!

(149)

How these irreps transform is given in TABLE
12
. We can see that
{\nu_{\mathrm{pr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}
transform in the same way as
{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}
, TABLE
5
. The cross term
|{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\delta}-\frac{r_{\!\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{2}}{r_{\!\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}r_{\!\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}}{\nu_{\mathrm{pr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}|^{2}
can be used to break the continuous symmetries in
\mathcal{V}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}
(
135
), (
136
). To achieve our alignment
\langle\phi_{\mathrm{b}}\rangle
(
.3
), we assign

\langle\nu_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle=\frac{r_{\!\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(\omega,1)^{\scriptscriptstyle T},\quad\langle\nu_{\mathrm{r}}\rangle=\frac{r_{\!\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(1,1)^{\scriptscriptstyle T},

(150)

so that the above-mentioned cross term vanishes. The stabilizers of
\langle\nu_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle
and
\langle\nu_{\mathrm{r}}\rangle
, TABLE
10
, fully determine them up to their norms. Hence, symmetry arguments guarantee that these vevs are the stationary points of the potentials of
\nu_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\nu_{\mathrm{r}}
, respectively.

To construct the general potential involving

and the driving flavons
\nu_{\mathrm{p}}
,
\nu_{\mathrm{q}}
and

,

and

, TABLE

. Adding all such cross terms, we construct the general potential,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{\small$\mathcal{V}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}\nu}$}&\text{\small$=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}\nu}+c_{47}{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}{\nu_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}+c_{48}{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}{\nu_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha}+c_{49}{\phi_{\mathrm{b}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}{\nu_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!2\alpha}$}\\

Using arguments similar to those provided earlier, we can show that all these cross terms are compatible. Therefore, the general renormalizable potential

coefficients

and

arbitrary constants

The potential involving

This potential has a

-sphere as its minimum, with
\tilde{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}=O(4)_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}
. To break
\tilde{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}
, we can utilize driving flavons transforming as triplets of
S_{4}
that couple with the
S_{4}
triplets obtained from
\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\otimes\xi_{\mathrm{c}}
. However, such driving flavons will produce dangerous cross terms involving the
S_{4}
triplet
\chi
, which would spoil our vevs. The solution to avoid this problem is to introduce driving flavons that are not
S_{4}
triplets but whose tensor products with themselves generate the required
S_{4}
triplets. We propose that these flavons transform as quartets of
Q_{8}\rtimes S_{4}
, generated by
\mathbf{T}
,
\mathbf{R}
and the auxiliary generator
\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{\lambda}}
. The representation matrices
\mathbf{t}_{4}
,
\mathbf{r}_{4}
and
{\mathbf{x}}_{\lambda}
, corresponding to the abstract elements
\mathbf{T}
,
\mathbf{R}
and
\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{\lambda}}
, respectively, that act on these flavons are given by

\mathbf{t}_{4}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&\mathbf{t}_{3}\end{matrix}\right)\!,\,\,\,\mathbf{r}_{4}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&\mathbf{r}_{3}\end{matrix}\right)\!,\,\,\,{\mathbf{x}}_{\lambda}=\left(\begin{matrix}-i\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!y}&0\\
0&i\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!y}\end{matrix}\right)\!.\!\!

(179)

These matrices generate the faithful quartet real representation of the group
Q_{8}\rtimes S_{4}
. The aforementioned solution to prevent dangerous cross terms was introduced in
Holthausen and Schmidt 2012
in relation to an
A_{4}
-based model with the help of quartets of
Q_{8}\rtimes A_{4}
. Table 2 in
Holthausen and Schmidt 2012
lists
Q_{8}\rtimes S_{4}
as one of the groups relevant to
S_{4}
-based models.

The tensor product of
x=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
and
y=(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
transforming as quartets of
Q_{8}\rtimes S_{4}
is given by

x\otimes y=(xy)_{\boldsymbol{1}}\oplus(xy)_{\boldsymbol{3}_{1}}\oplus(xy)_{\boldsymbol{6}}\oplus(xy)_{\boldsymbol{3}}\oplus(xy)_{\boldsymbol{3}_{2}},

(180)

where
(xy)_{\boldsymbol{1}}=x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}y
is the invariant singlet and

\displaystyle(xy)_{\boldsymbol{3}_{1}}

\displaystyle=(x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!z}\otimes{\scriptstyle{\mathbf{I}}}_{2})y,x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}({\scriptstyle{\mathbf{I}}}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!z})y,x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!z}\otimes\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!z})y)^{\scriptscriptstyle T},

\displaystyle\begin{split}(xy)_{\boldsymbol{6}}&=(x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!z}\otimes\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!x})y,x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}({\scriptstyle{\mathbf{I}}}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!x})y,x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!x}\otimes\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!z})y,\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,\,x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!x}\otimes{\scriptstyle{\mathbf{I}}}_{2})y,x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}(i\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!y}\otimes i\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!y})y,x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!x}\otimes\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!x})y)^{\scriptscriptstyle T},\end{split}

\displaystyle(xy)_{\boldsymbol{3}}

\displaystyle=(x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}({\scriptstyle{\mathbf{I}}}_{2}\otimes i\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!y})y,x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}(i\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!y}\otimes\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!z})y,x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}(i\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!y}\otimes\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!x})y)^{\scriptscriptstyle T},

\displaystyle(xy)_{\boldsymbol{3}_{2}}

\displaystyle=(x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!z}\otimes i\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!y})y,x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}(i\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!y}\otimes{\scriptstyle{\mathbf{I}}}_{2})y,x^{\scriptscriptstyle T}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!x}\otimes i\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!y})y)^{\scriptscriptstyle T}.

These irreps transform as given in TABLE
13
. The triplet
(xy)_{\boldsymbol{3}}
obtained from the antisymmetric part of the tensor product transforms as
\mathbf{t}_{3}
and
\mathbf{r}_{3}
under
\mathbf{T}
and
\mathbf{R}
, respectively, and remains invariant under
\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{\lambda}}
. Hence, it is well suited for breaking
\tilde{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}=O(4)_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}
.

Table 14:
The transformation rules for the driving flavons
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
and the irreps obtained from them.

We introduce the flavons
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
that transform as quartets of
Q_{8}\rtimes S_{4}
, TABLE
14
. The flavons
\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
change sign under
\mathbf{Z}_{\nu}
and
\mathbf{X}
, respectively. In preparation to constructing the potentials involving
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
, we define the group element
\mathbf{E}_{\lambda}=\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{\lambda}}^{2}
. As shown in TABLE
11
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
change sign under
\mathbf{E}_{\lambda}
while every other flavon remains invariant under it. Hence,
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
can appear in cross terms as quadratic products only. We define
{\lambda_{\mathrm{x}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}=(\lambda_{\mathrm{x}}\lambda_{\mathrm{x}})_{\boldsymbol{3}_{1}}
,
{\lambda_{\mathrm{xy}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}=(\lambda_{\mathrm{x}}\lambda_{\mathrm{y}})_{\boldsymbol{3}_{1}}
,
{\lambda_{\mathrm{x}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}=(\lambda_{\mathrm{x}}\lambda_{\mathrm{x}})_{\boldsymbol{6}}
and
{\lambda_{\mathrm{xy}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}=(\lambda_{\mathrm{x}}\lambda_{\mathrm{y}})_{\boldsymbol{3}}
with
\mathrm{x}
,
\mathrm{y}
being equal to
\mathrm{p}
,
\mathrm{q}
or
\mathrm{r}
. Besides the norms, i.e.,
|\lambda_{\mathrm{x}}|^{2}
, the only quadratic-order irreps that remain invariant under
\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{\lambda}}
are
\lambda_{\mathrm{x}}^{\scriptscriptstyle T}\lambda_{\mathrm{y}}
and
{\lambda_{\mathrm{xy}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}
. We list them in TABLE
14
. The irreps
{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\!\!\!2\beta}
and
{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\!\!\!2\gamma}
, TABLE
5
, transform in the same way as
{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}
and
{\lambda_{\mathrm{pr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}
, respectively. We can break
\tilde{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}
by coupling these together using two cross terms
|{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\!\!\!2\beta}-\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}|^{2}
and
|{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\!\!\!2\gamma}-\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{pr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}|^{2}
.

We make the following assignments of vevs:

\displaystyle\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle

\displaystyle=r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}(1,0,0,0)^{\scriptscriptstyle T},

(181)

\displaystyle\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle

\displaystyle=r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}(0,0,1,0)^{\scriptscriptstyle T},

(182)

\displaystyle\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}}{\sqrt{2}}(0,1,0,1)^{\scriptscriptstyle T}.

(183)

These are fully defined, up to their norms, by their stabilizers, TABLE
10
; hence, they are guaranteed to be stationary points of the corresponding potentials. It can be shown that the two cross terms mentioned earlier vanish at the above vevs and
\langle\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\rangle
(
.3
). We now proceed to explicitly construct the potentials of
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
to show that the above vevs are obtained as a minimum.

Using
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
, we construct

\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}

=c_{55}m^{2}|\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}+c_{56}|\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}|^{4}

(184)

\displaystyle\text{\small$=k_{45}(|\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}-r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2})^{2}-k_{45}r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{4}$}.

(185)

\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}
has a
3
-sphere as its minimum, with the symmetry group
\breve{T}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}=O(4)_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}
. The general potential of
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
is given by

\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}

=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}+c_{57}|{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}|^{2}

(186)

\displaystyle\text{\small$=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}+k_{46}|{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}|^{2}$}.

(187)

The symmetry group of
\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}
is
((Z_{2_{1}}\times Z_{2_{2}}\times Z_{2_{3}}\times Z_{2_{4}})\rtimes S_{4})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}
generated by the sign changes as well as the permutations of the four components of
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
. In other words, the term
|{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}|^{2}
(
186
) breaks
\breve{T}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}
to
\acute{G}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}=((Z_{2_{1}}\times Z_{2_{2}}\times Z_{2_{3}}\times Z_{2_{4}})\rtimes S_{4})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}
. Note that
\acute{G}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}
is larger than
Q_{8}\rtimes S_{4}
because of accidental symmetries.

The alignment
\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle
(
181
) remains invariant under the sign changes as well as the permutations of the last three components of
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
, which generates the group
(S_{4}\times Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}
, i.e.,
\acute{H}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}=(S_{4}\times Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}
forms the stabilizer of
\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle
under
\acute{G}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}
. Since
\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle
is fully determined by its stabilizer in the
3
-sphere, symmetry arguments guarantee that it forms a stationary point. The rewritten potential (
187
) shows that we can obtain it as a minimum. The solution of
|\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}|=r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}
and
{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}=0
is a discrete set of points, with
\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle
being one among them. We have
\tilde{H}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}=\acute{H}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}=(S_{4}\times Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}
.

The construction of the potential of
\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}
follows the same lines as that of
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
. We construct

\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}

=c_{58}m^{2}|\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2}+c_{59}|\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}|^{4}

(188)

\displaystyle\text{\small$=k_{47}(|\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2}-r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{2})^{2}-k_{47}r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{4}$},

(189)

\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}

=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}+c_{60}|{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}|^{2}

(190)

\displaystyle\text{\small$=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}+k_{48}|{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}|^{2}$}.

(191)

\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}
has a discrete minimum at
\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle
(
182
) with
\tilde{H}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}=(S_{4}\times Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}
, generated by the sign changes and permutations of the first, second and fourth elements of
\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}
.

Combining
\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}
, we construct

\text{\small$\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}=\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}+\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}$},

(192)

where
\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}
denotes the combined multiplet
(\lambda_{\mathrm{p}},\lambda_{\mathrm{q}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
.
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}
has a minimum at
\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}=(\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle,\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle)^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
with
\tilde{H}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}=\tilde{H}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}\times\tilde{H}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}=(S_{4}\times Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}\times(S_{4}\times Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}
. To obtain the general potential involving
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}
, we need to include their cross terms. There are three independent cross terms between
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}
. By including them, we construct

\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}

=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}\!+c_{61}{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}+c_{62}{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}\!+c_{63}|\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}|\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2}\!\!\!\!

(193)

\displaystyle\text{\small$=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}\!+k_{49}|{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}|^{2}\!+k_{50}(\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}^{\scriptscriptstyle T}\lambda_{\mathrm{q}})^{2}\!+k_{51}(|\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}\!-\frac{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{2}}|\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2})^{2}$}.

(194)

Let us determine if the three cross terms in (
193
) are compatible with
\tilde{H}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}
. First, we consider the cross term
{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}
. With
\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}
being the eight component multiplet
(({\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1},{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2},{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}_{3},{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}_{4}),({\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1},{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2},{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}_{3},{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}_{4}))^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
, a point in
\text{fix}(\tilde{H}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}})
is given by
(a(1,0,0,0),b(0,0,1,0))^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
where
a
and
b
are real numbers. It can be shown that the gradient of
{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}
calculated at the above-mentioned point is
(-2ab^{2}(1,0,0,0),-2a^{2}b(0,0,1,0))^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
. Since the gradient also lies in
\text{fix}(\tilde{H}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}})
, the term
{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}
is compatible with
\tilde{H}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}
. For the case of
{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}
, we can show that both
{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}
and
{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}
vanish at
(a(1,0,0,0),b(0,0,1,0))^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
. Hence, according to corollary B in
Krishnan 2023
, the term
{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}
is compatible with
\tilde{H}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}
. The term
|\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}|\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2}
, which is the product of norms, is also compatible. Therefore,
\tilde{H}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}
is unaffected by the addition of the cross terms, i.e.,
\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle
and
\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle
remain unspoiled. This is made apparent in (
194
). Both
{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}
and
(\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}^{\scriptscriptstyle T}\lambda_{\mathrm{q}})
vanish at our vevs.
|{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}|^{2}
and
(\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}^{\scriptscriptstyle T}\lambda_{\mathrm{q}})^{2}\!
can be expressed as linear combinations of the three earlier-mentioned cross terms.
\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}
(
194
) has
9
arbitrary constants
r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}},r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}},k_{45},..,k_{51}
which are functions of the
9
coefficients
c_{55},..,c_{63}
in (
193
).

Using
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
, we construct

\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}

=c_{64}m^{2}|\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}|^{2}+c_{65}|\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}|^{4},

(195)

=k_{52}(|\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}|^{2}-r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{2})^{2}-k_{52}r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{4}

(196)

\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}
has a
3
-sphere as its minimum with the symmetry group
\tilde{T}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}=O(4)_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}
. We may add
|{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}|^{2}
to
\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}
to break
O(4)_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}
to the discrete group
((Z_{2_{1}}\times Z_{2_{2}}\times Z_{2_{3}}\times Z_{2_{4}})\rtimes S_{4})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}
. Our alignment
\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}\rangle
(
183
) is fully determined by its stabilizer under this discrete group. Therefore, in the
3
-sphere,
\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}\rangle
is guaranteed to appear as a stationary point of the newly obtained potential. However, it so happens that this stationary point can never be a minimum. Rather, it is a saddle point for every choice of the coefficients. In the discussion below, we utilize the cross terms of
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
with
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}
along with the term
|{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}|^{2}
to break
\tilde{T}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}=O(4)_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}
and obtain a minimum for
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
.

First, we combine
\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}
to obtain,

\text{\small$\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}=\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}+\mathcal{V}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}$},

(197)

where
\lambda=(\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}},\lambda_{\mathrm{r}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}=(\lambda_{\mathrm{p}},\lambda_{\mathrm{q}},\lambda_{\mathrm{r}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
.
\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}
has a minimum consisting of
\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle
,
\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle
and a
3
-sphere for
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
. For this minimum, we have the symmetry groups
\breve{H}_{\lambda}=\tilde{H}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}=(S_{4}\times Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}\times(S_{4}\times Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}
and
\breve{T}_{\lambda}=\tilde{T}_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}=O(4)_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}
. We add two cross terms in addition to the term
|{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}|^{2}
to
\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}
, resulting in

\text{\small$\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}+c_{66}|{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}|^{2}\!+c_{67}{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}+c_{68}{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}$}.

(198)

These terms break
\breve{T}_{\lambda}=O(4)_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}
to
\acute{G}_{\lambda}=((Z_{2_{1}}\times Z_{2_{2}}\times Z_{2_{3}}\times Z_{2_{4}})\rtimes Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}
generated by the sign changes of the four components of
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
and the permutation of its second and fourth components. The alignment
\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}\rangle
(
183
) breaks
\acute{G}_{\lambda}
to
\acute{H}_{\lambda}=((Z_{2_{1}}\times Z_{2_{3}})\rtimes Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}
. Since
\acute{H}_{\lambda}
fully determines
\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}\rangle
in the
3
-sphere, we are guaranteed to obtain it as a stationary point. We obtain
\tilde{H}_{\lambda}=\breve{H}_{\lambda}\times\acute{H}_{\lambda}=(S_{4}\times Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}\times(S_{4}\times Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}\times((Z_{2_{1}}\times Z_{2_{3}})\rtimes Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}
. We can show that the stationary point forms a minimum by rewriting
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}
(
198
) as

\begin{split}\text{\small$\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}$}&\text{\small$=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}+k_{53}|2{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}+\frac{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}+\frac{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{2}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\alpha}|^{2}$}\\
&\quad\text{\small$+k_{54}|{\lambda_{\mathrm{pr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}|^{2}+k_{54}(\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}^{\scriptscriptstyle T}\lambda_{\mathrm{r}})^{2}+\frac{k_{54}}{2}\frac{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{2}}(|\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}|^{2}-\frac{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}|\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2})^{2}$}\\
&\quad\text{\small$+k_{55}|{\lambda_{\mathrm{qr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}|^{2}+k_{55}(\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle T}\lambda_{\mathrm{r}})^{2}+\frac{k_{55}}{2}\frac{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{2}}(|\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}|^{2}-\frac{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{2}}|\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2})^{2}$}.\end{split}

(199)

The
14
arbitrary constants
r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}},r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}},r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}},k_{45},..,k_{55}
in (
199
) can be obtained as functions of the
14
coefficients
c_{55},..,c_{68}
in (
198
).
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}
attains its minimum when each term in (
199
) vanishes. Our vev
\langle\lambda\rangle=(\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}\rangle,\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle,\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}\rangle)^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
(
181
)-(
183
) constitutes one of the points (among a discrete set) where this happens.

The general potential involving these driving flavons is obtained by including four more cross terms of
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
with
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{\small$\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}$}&\text{\small$=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}+c_{69}{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}+c_{70}{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}$}\\
&\text{\small$\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+c_{71}|\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}|\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}|^{2}+c_{72}|\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2}|\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}|^{2}$}.\end{split}

(200)

A point in
\text{fix}(\tilde{H}_{\lambda})
is given by
\lambda=(\lambda_{\mathrm{p}},\lambda_{\mathrm{q}},\lambda_{\mathrm{r}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}=(a(1,0,0,0),b(0,0,1,0),\frac{c}{\sqrt{2}}(0,1,0,1))^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
where
a
,
b
and
c
are real numbers. Let us analyze the cross term
{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}
. Since it involves only
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
, we may consider the point

(\lambda_{\mathrm{p}},\lambda_{\mathrm{r}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}=(a(1,0,0,0),\frac{c}{\sqrt{2}}(0,1,0,1))^{\scriptscriptstyle T}.

(201)

We can show that at this point, the gradient of
{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}
is
((0,0,0,0),(0,0,0,0))^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
, which corresponds to (
201
) with
a\rightarrow 0
,
c\rightarrow 0
, i.e., the gradient calculated at
\text{fix}(\tilde{H}_{\lambda})
lies in
\text{fix}(\tilde{H}_{\lambda})
. Therefore, the cross term
{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}
is compatible with
\tilde{H}_{\lambda}
. Similarly, the gradient of
{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{\!\!\!\!2\beta}
at
(\lambda_{\mathrm{q}},\lambda_{\mathrm{r}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}=(b(0,0,1,0),\frac{c}{\sqrt{2}}(0,1,0,1))^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
vanishes, proving that it is compatible
\tilde{H}_{\lambda}
. The cross terms
|\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}|\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}|^{2}
and
|\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2}|\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}|^{2}
, which are products of norms, are also compatible. Hence,
\tilde{H}_{\lambda}
is preserved, and the vevs (
181
)-(
183
) form a minimum of
\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}
(
200
). To make this apparent, we rewrite
\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{\small$\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}$}&\text{\small$=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\lambda}+k_{56}(\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}^{\scriptscriptstyle T}\lambda_{\mathrm{r}})^{2}+k_{57}(|\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}|^{2}-\frac{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}|\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2})^{2}$}\\
&\text{\small$\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+k_{58}(\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle T}\lambda_{\mathrm{r}})^{2}+k_{59}(|\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}|^{2}-\frac{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{2}}|\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2})^{2}$}.\end{split}

(202)

\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}
(
200
) contains
18
coefficients, and correspondingly
\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}
(
202
) contains
18
arbitrary constants.

We combine the potential of the flavon
\xi_{\mathrm{c}}
with that of its driving flavons
\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}
and
\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}
to obtain

\text{\small$\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}=\mathcal{V}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}+\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}$},

(203)

where
\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda
denotes the combined multiplet. A minimum of this potential corresponds to a
3
-sphere for
\xi_{\mathrm{c}}
and (
181
)-(
183
) for
\lambda
. We have
\breve{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}=\tilde{H}_{\lambda}
and
\breve{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}=O(4)_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}
. We break
\breve{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}
by coupling
\xi_{\mathrm{c}}
with the driving flavons,

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}

=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}+c_{73}\,{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\!\!\!2\beta\,{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}+c_{74}\,{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\!\!\!2\gamma\,{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{pr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}

(204)

\displaystyle\text{\small$=\breve{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}+k_{60}|{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\!\!\!2\beta}\!-\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{pq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}|^{2}+k_{61}|{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\!\!\!2\gamma}\!-\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{pr}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}|^{2}$}.

(205)

The cross terms in (
204
) break
\breve{T}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}
to
\acute{G}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}=(K_{4}\times Z_{2})_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}
generated by

,

and
\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\rightarrow-\xi_{\mathrm{c}}
. The stabilizer of
\langle\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\rangle
(
.3
) under

is given by
\acute{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}=K_{4\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}
, TABLE
6
, generated by

and
\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\rightarrow i\boldsymbol{\tau}_{2}\xi_{\mathrm{c}}^{*}
. This stabilizer fully determines
\langle\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\rangle
in the
3
-sphere; therefore, symmetry arguments guarantee that it forms a stationary point. We obtain
\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}=\acute{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}\times\breve{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}=K_{4\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}\times(S_{4}\times Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}\times(S_{4}\times Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}\times((Z_{2_{1}}\times Z_{2_{3}})\rtimes Z_{2})_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}
. The existence of a minimum as the stationary point is made apparent in (

). At

,
\langle\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}\rangle
,

) vanishes.
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}
has
22
coefficients and correspondingly
22
arbitrary coefficients.

The general potential constructed with
\xi_{\mathrm{c}}
and its driving flavons is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{\small$\mathcal{V}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}$}&\text{\small$=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}+c_{75}\,{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha\,{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{rq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}+c_{76}|\xi_{\mathrm{c}}|^{2}|\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2}$}\\
&\text{\small$\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+c_{77}|\xi_{\mathrm{c}}|^{2}|\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2}+c_{78}|\xi_{\mathrm{c}}|^{2}|\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}|^{2}$}\end{split}

(206)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{\small$=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}+k_{62}|{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha}\!-\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{rq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}|^{2}\!+k_{63}(|\xi_{\mathrm{c}}|^{2}\!-\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}}^{2}}|\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}|^{2})^{2}$}\\
&\text{\small$\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 9.24994pt\,+k_{64}(|\xi_{\mathrm{c}}|^{2}\!-\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}^{2}}|\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}|^{2})^{2}+k_{65}(|\xi_{\mathrm{c}}|^{2}\!-\frac{r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{2}}{r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}^{2}}|\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}|^{2})^{2}$}\end{split}

(207)

To analyze the cross term
{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha\,{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{rq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}
, we consider the multiplet
(\xi_{\mathrm{c}},\lambda_{\mathrm{q}},\lambda_{\mathrm{r}})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}=(({\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}_{1r},{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}_{1i},{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}_{2r},{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}_{2i}),

({\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}_{1},{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}_{2},{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}_{3},{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}}_{4}),({\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}_{1},{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}_{2},{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}_{3},{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}}_{4}))^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
. In this basis, a general point in
\text{fix}(\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda})
is given by

\text{\small$(\frac{a}{\sqrt{2}}(\cos\frac{\pi}{8},\sin\frac{\pi}{8},\cos\frac{3\pi}{8},\sin\frac{3\pi}{8}),b(0,0,1,0),\frac{c}{\sqrt{2}}(0,1,0,1))^{\scriptscriptstyle T}$}.

(208)

At this point, we obtain the gradient of the cross term
{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}}^{\!\!\!2\alpha\,{\scriptscriptstyle T}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{rq}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}
as
(\sqrt{2}abc(\cos\frac{\pi}{8},\sin\frac{\pi}{8},\cos\frac{3\pi}{8},\sin\frac{3\pi}{8}),

, which lies in
\text{fix}(\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda})
. Therefore, the cross term is compatible with
\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}
. The other cross terms in (
206
) are products of norms; hence, they also are compatible. Therefore,
\tilde{H}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}
is preserved, and we obtain our assigned vevs as one of the minima of
\mathcal{V}_{\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\lambda}
(
206
). This is made explicit in (
207
). (
206
) and (
207
) contain
26
coefficients
c_{53},..,c_{78}
and

arbitrary constants
r_{\!\xi_{\mathrm{c}}},r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{p}}},r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{q}}},r_{\lambda_{\mathrm{r}}},k_{44},..,k_{65}
, respectively.

D.6
The potential involving
\phi_{\mathrm{a}}

The general potential for
\phi_{\mathrm{a}}
is given by

\mathcal{V}_{\phi_{\mathrm{a}}}

=c_{79}\,m^{2}|\phi_{\mathrm{a}}|^{2}+c_{80}|\phi_{\mathrm{a}}|^{4}

(209)

=k_{66}(|\phi_{\mathrm{a}}|^{2}-r_{\!\phi_{\mathrm{a}}}^{2})^{2}-k_{66}r_{\!\phi_{\mathrm{a}}}^{4}.

(210)

It has a
3
-sphere as its minimum with the symmetry group
\tilde{T}_{\phi_{\mathrm{a}}}=O(4)_{\phi_{\mathrm{a}}}
. To break
\tilde{T}_{\phi_{\mathrm{a}}}
, we introduce an auxiliary generator
\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{\rho}}
and four driving flavons that transform under it: a complex doublet
\rho_{\mathrm{p}}=({\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1},{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2})^{\scriptscriptstyle T}
, which is a real irrep of dimension four, and three complex singlets
\rho_{\mathrm{q}}
,
\rho_{\mathrm{r}}
and
\rho_{\mathrm{s}}
, which are real irreps of dimension two each. How they transform is given in TABLE
15
. The tensor products of the
2O
doublets
\phi_{\mathrm{a}}
and
\xi_{\mathrm{c}}
with
\rho_{\mathrm{p}}
and
\rho_{\mathrm{s}}
are given by

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathrm{a}}\!\otimes\rho_{\mathrm{p}}

\displaystyle={\phi_{\mathrm{a\rho}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}\oplus{\phi_{\mathrm{a\rho}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\beta}\oplus{\phi_{\mathrm{a\rho}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma},

(211)

\displaystyle\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\!\otimes\rho_{\mathrm{s}}

\displaystyle={\xi_{\mathrm{c\rho}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}\oplus{\xi_{\mathrm{c\rho}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\beta},

(212)

where

\displaystyle{\phi_{\mathrm{a\rho}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\phi_{\mathrm{a}}\rrbracket\rho_{\mathrm{p}},\,\,\,\,\,\qquad{\phi_{\mathrm{a\rho}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\beta}=\llbracket\phi_{\mathrm{a}}\rrbracket\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\!z}\rho_{\mathrm{p}},

(213)

\displaystyle{\phi_{\mathrm{a\rho}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\gamma}

\displaystyle=({\phi_{\mathrm{a}}}_{1}{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2},{\phi_{\mathrm{a}}}_{2}{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}_{2},{\phi_{\mathrm{a}}}_{2}^{\!\!*}{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1},-{\phi_{\mathrm{a}}}_{1}^{\!\!*}{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}_{1})^{\scriptscriptstyle T},

(214)

\displaystyle{\xi_{\mathrm{c\rho}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\alpha}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\rrbracket^{\scriptscriptstyle T}\llbracket\rho_{\mathrm{s}}\rrbracket,\qquad{\xi_{\mathrm{c\rho}}}^{\!\!\!\!\!\!2\beta}=\llbracket\xi_{\mathrm{c}}\rrbracket^{\scriptscriptstyle T}\llbracket\rho_{\mathrm{s}}\rrbracket^{*},

(215)

with

, which is the flavon
\rho_{\mathrm{s}}
expressed in the placeholder notation. How these objects transform is given in TABLE

.

Table 15:
The transformation rules for the driving flavons
\rho_{\mathrm{p}}
,

,

and
